#8609 1 2006-05-10T19:56:01Z lord_day
Here we go. I have supplied a map, and the point of this is to get as many points as possible. However it has to be played as a hold left DDA, and the only changes that are alowed to made are to TILES. There are no tiles in this map, and you can add as many as you want. However, NO OBJECT EDITING/MOVING/DELETEING/ADDING/ETC.

The scoring system works as follows:

1)you start with 20 points.
2) Every tile you place takes away 1 point.
3) Every gold piece you get adds 2 points
4)add 10 points for completing the level.
5) plus one point for evey trap door open/closed.

Good idea from Evi_Bob, send me a message with your code ao no-one else steals your ideas. Player with the most points wins! You may submit as many maps as you want, within a limit, but only one map will be on the leader board. Good luck.

map: http://numa.notdot.net/map/41983

Leader Board:
Evil_Bob 41 points
scythe33 39 points
VV33 36 points
Beanhead 35 points


Please tell me if you think I have made a mistake with your points.

#181254 4 2006-05-28T02:13:31Z lord_day
here are two types of fake tiles:

Number 1:
firstly load a blank map and put a block in.
then press \ and find in the code the 1 among the 0's.
change the 1 to a . then press load. now you can see the tile, but N goes through it.

Number 2:
load an empty map. add anywhere in the code a Z then load. you get a tile that behaves really wierdly.... experiment!!!

Number 3:
The problem with number 1 is that if you copy the code and then press J, then load the code you copied, the tile is now gone. I want to know how to make a tile that behaves like number 1 except is contained in the code. I have seen this done, but i need help

Well if you can get him to play that would be awesome. So your definetly in the team? Thats cool. Basically this is an international competition, where each team has 2 mapmakers and 2 highscorers. The details havent really been worked out but it will work like this:

1) matches are played one team vs. another
2) The mapmakers are given a specifaction for their maps, and they have some time to build them.
3) The maps are submited and the highscorers have a week to get the highest high score they can get on all 4 maps. ( btw, i think the demo should be done in user levels so gold gives you points)
4) the other countries all rate the maps, and as combination of the highscorers score and the mapmakers score, you get a winner.

I think that does it..... oh, and would you like our teams flag? its in my sig.

#192984 32 2006-06-22T15:29:14Z lord_day
Nice first map. My suggestions for maps in general:

Don't put too many enemies. This is my biggest problem, too many enemies. If you want it to be hard, one well placed enemy can dominate the whole map.

Has been said, but don't build rooms, they are nooby.

Make a tileset that is not akward to move on, doesn't have to have major flow, just don't let it impede the level. This is a little hard, but will come later me thinks.....

No hidden traps or spamming of objects. Like Blizzy said, if you have four mines on a 1/4 snap, where a 1/2 snap would work, put them on a 1/2 snap.

TESTING AND PLAYTESTING. too many noobs build a map in neditor and submit it to numa without playing it. play it and change anything you don't like. Don't be afraid to completely redo part of your map if it doesn't feel right.

maybe more will come later.........

#194047 3 2006-06-24T21:31:33Z lord_day
Well, here I am sharing out my secrets . I actually was told this in another topic I started .

The way I made that level was quite complicated, and I had to use microsoft word. I'll give you some step by step instructions (the punctuation in quotation marks is what you actually use, not punctuation)

1) build the whole tileset, putting an obscure tile where you want the fake tiles to be later. I used the quarter circles that point down to the right. ( the S under 8).

2) Take the level data so far, making sure there are NO objects, and paste it into Microsoft word. Then press ctrl F and use the replace function to replace every character that represents the obscure tile ( mine was '-') and replace it with a '.'

3) copy and paste the new data into the neditor box and load the level. There will now be blank spaces where the other tile was before. Pressing H before loading will fill these tiles but not put anything there.

This technique is very hard if you want to edit the tileset, but you'll manage.

What I've written is very confusing..... tell me if it doesn't work/ you don't understand.

x equals 0.999999999.....
10x equals 9.99999.......
Take them away from eachother. Then:
9x = 9
Therefore x = 1

That proves it. Got it?
#194225 10 2006-06-25T10:20:33Z lord_day
But thats the point. There are no last digits. Otherwise it wouldn't be 0.9 recurring. I think another proof is that two numbers have to have another number between them to be different. 0.9999999999999999... cannot be rounded up (which is probably the method some people are using), because for example:

0.08673452 could equal 0.086735 because you use the end of the number to do so.

0.99999999... can't be rounded up because there is no end to the 'number'.

If you read the proofs, no-one has rounded up, that would not be a proof if they just rounded up. Read the proofs, 0.99999.... does equal 1.

#194344 29 2006-06-25T14:56:10Z lord_day
Ok, take two groups of numbers, one groups is every positive integer, the other group is every positive even integer. Both groups have infinite numbers, yet every number in the even group can be paired up with a number in the other group, leaving some numbers in the first group un-paired. This shows that there are different sizes of infinity. If there are different sizes of infinity, there must be infinite different sizes of infinity, so ∞ = ∞ + 1 can be true.

#194548 42 2006-06-25T22:08:59Z lord_day
Quote: wedgie, June 25, 2006 09:47 pm
Regardless of your workings here; the more decimal places you take 0.9 to will just make it closer and closer to 1, but it will never actually be 1.

If 0.9 recurring equals 1, then why isn't 0.9 just written as 1 in the first place? Oh yeah, that's because it doesn't equal 1, it equals slightly less that it.
Have you ever written 0.999.....? no. So it is written as 1.

And in terms of decimal places, its not a matter of how close you get. For it to be 0.99999..... you have to take ALL the decimal places. So its not just getting closer, it starts where you're actually there.

There are so many proofs for 0.999... equalling one, ranging from simple algebra to complex formulas and theoretical ideas that you can't say its not true. It may seem strange but 0.9999.... = 1. I can prove to you that it does, but you can't prove that it doesn't. Anything maths you apply to it will make it turn out as 1.

The quality or condition of being infinite.
Unbounded space, time, or quantity.

#220641 14 2006-08-27T19:49:32Z lord_day
To make a thwump go backwards from right to left:

Place a thwump half way in a wall.
Go to the code for the thwump, it will look like this: 8^132,112.
Change the first number to one smaller: 8^131,112.

That should work.

#252746 4 2006-11-10T23:12:07Z lord_day
Basically, we split six people into 3 teams (note any number of teams can be made). So the game starts. One person on your team spends two minutes (excatly) on a map. After two minutes they send the data to their team mate, who spends another two minutes (exactly) editing/adding to the map. The data is sent back to the first person, and its is added again. This happens until each person has edited the map 3 teams, giving a total of 12 minutes. That is a twelve minute team tag. I think that is clearer.

#12360 1 2006-11-19T22:06:16Z lord_day
The Completed Column

The Completed Column is the culmination of my work for the past few weeks. It involves 51 levels, of all difficulty, ranging from so easy you could do it blindfolded, to nigh impossible. Every level after 08-0 (inclusive) is very hard, so you can pat yourself on the back everytime you complete one. I'd love to see demos from any level, and I will compete with anyone for fastest times if they want to. (I never said I'd win!) Just to let you know, I've completed every level, so you should all be able to do so to, with a bit of patience.

The two people who have helped me most on this pack are rocket_thumped, and Wedgie. For that they each get a dedicated level, as well as the whole pack being dedicated to them:

rocket_thumped

He play tested everylevel, from 00-0 to 07-4. Without him, this pack would only be half the quality it is now. For that he is awarded 02-0: What have we left for our rockets?

I love you man, keep on rocking.

Wedgie

Wedgie played every level in the pack to make sure it wasn't cheatable, and also play tested 08-0 to 09-5 for me. He has insane skills, but even he was put to the test on some of the levels . Without him, many of these levels would be cheatable, so for his help he is awarded 07-3: He's a pirate

Wedgie, you are teh OMG! when it comes to playing N. Keep going dude.




And without further ado, the pack. Enjoy.

#12874 1 2006-12-15T13:22:46Z lord_day
Ok, the idea of this contest is that you have to collect as much gold as possible from the map below, placing as few objects as possible. The map will be watched like a DDA. I will be the only judge to this contest, and you all have two weeks to do the best you can.

The Rules

-Can be hold left, hold right or just do nothing. Must be specified when submitting the data.

-You can only add objects, except another ninja, more gold or another exit. No code editing is permitted except glitched doors.

-You cannot edit anything in the map that is already there.

-Every piece of gold collected gives you 3 points.

-The first object used subtracts 1 point. The second used subtracts 2 points. The third used subtracts 3 points. etc.

-Getting the exit key is worth 10 points. Completeting the level is worth 30 points.

-Map Data must be PM'd to me before the 29th of December. You may replace your submission only twice.


That is all. Any questions, just ask me here.
There will be a dedication for first place, and the right to name the place you came in your sig for everyone.
Get mapping

Spiker - 103 points
apg - 64 points
geti - 54 points

#273823 5 2006-12-20T12:24:45Z lord_day
Interesting idea there but I'm afraid not very good. Let me give you some tips:

1)Try to reduce the number of objects used. It massively increases loading time and makes the game slower to play. Not fun. (This includes the mass of gold used at the beginning)
2)Don't hide objects behind bounce blocks, or anything for that matter. It turns N into a guessing game. Also not fun.
3) Really think about your game play. The way to think is not: 'They will love this map because it's really hard' but more 'They will love this map because its really fun.'
4) Try to make more interesting tile sets. Instead of just making walls to restrict the ninja's movement, make tiles that aid it.
5) Kind of been covered, but don't place more enemies than required. It reduces the fun of the map in multiple ways.
6) Finally, check out NUMA for some map ideas. There you can find some amazing maps to help show you what a good map plays like.

So hopefully that should help. I look forward to your future maps

As said already but not very well.

Set A contains all even numbers.

Set B contains all odd numbers.

Set C contains all integers.

Set D contains all numbers.

All these sets have infinite numbers, yet:

Set A < Set C < Set D

Cool map. It's fun for a first. I assume that you dont have an account on NUMA; otherwise submitting maps is really easy. Go to the configure menu in N, and make an account on that. Then go to NUMA and sign in using that account and you'll get a confirmation email. Blah blah blah, its all very standard.

Once you have an account, go to submit a map, and place your level data in there. The bit you submit is the data without the tags. So, remove the $name#author#comment# and such. Um, if that doesn't help just ask again, telling us what your problem was.

#14007 1 2007-02-11T23:39:56Z lord_day
I had this thought, and it's only a rough idea right now, but I think that if custom highscores were to be implemented, it should be done in the following way. Each week, some 'judges' would select 5 levels that would have to be highscored. Then everyone would have a week to highscore it. There would be an online table that, perhaps, would show what times people where getting, but not let you watch their replay. At the end of the week, depending on the scoring system, the person who got the best times, would be the winner.

This idea, as I said is very rough, would keep highscoring fresh, and hopefully, like NTTS, focus more on innovation, rather than perfect runs. I've spoken to a few highscores, and some of them are finding highscoring boring, as there is only so much you can do among 500 levels. I can understand that this will be hard to implement, but I feel that it has benefits, such as hackers can be easily identified, and will have a less lasting affect.

So tell me what you think, and any potential problems you can see, and we will try and iron them out!

#302024 2 2007-03-01T13:51:18Z lord_day
This level pack was a mixed bag. Some of the levels, were frankly awful, like 02-0 The Broken Ladder. But on the other hand levels like 08-0 A Hail of Gunfire, were really amazing levels. Also, the fact that the 09-4 of the pack was really easy to complete was a let down. So my future suggestions are:

1) Take more time over your pack. The good levels in this pack were completely comprimised by the awful levels here. Never settle for an average level or a 'filler' in your pack if you can.

2) Work on the difficulty spacing of your pack. At first the difficulty spacing seemed pretty good, but it then started to fray as the pack progressed. Some of the 03-s were harder than the 06-s for example.

However, overall, it was pretty good for a first pack.

#14549 1 2007-03-24T00:31:39Z lord_day
Recently I have come across a few varied opinions about how the rating system should work. Im attempting to set this out in this best way possible, so we can sort this problem out. The question I am asking is:

If a person has an opinion on a map, is it wrong for them to vote against that opinion?

I think you should only vote what you think a map is worth, while others that I have talked to think you should be allowed to vote whatever you want, regardless of your opinion. I know this question is hard to answer, as there are no such rules implying this on NUMA, and even if there were, they would be hard to enforce, however, here is my opinion on the subject:

I think we have the rating system as a way to signal our opinions of a map to others, without going through the effort of posting a comment, which we dont always want to have to do. Rating against your opinion in my view, is almost like rating against the system. When rating maps, we should all try and rate on the same standard, so that ratings can be more meaningful. By filling in the gaps of the written indicators S&M supplied to us, I come to this:

0 = Perfectly Awful
0.5 = Amazingly Bad
1 = Very Bad
1.5 = Bad
2 = Below Average / Not Bad
2.5 = Average
3 = Above Average / Not Good
3.5 = Good
4 = Very Good
4.5 = Amazingly Good
5 = Perfect

I view the rating system in a symmetrical way, and believe that S&M gave us these written indicators as a means of unifying or ratings.

Also, a point to note out is that nowhere in this did I say peoples opinions have to be the same on all maps. That would be pointless, and ruin the voting system as well. All I am saying is that your rating should reflect your opinion on the map. I understand that I may be looking into this far too much, and that I have become to philosophical with my ideas, but its an issue that irks me.

Also, while I am here, I would like to ask people who give out average ratings of 4s and 4.5s to think about what they are rating. Is that map /really/ flawless? Is it /really/ very good? Remember, you are aiming to give out an average rating of 2.5, as 2.5 is the average rating.

So what are everyone elses opinions on the subject?

#308348 6 2007-03-28T18:22:27Z lord_day
Quote
Moving Walls

One very special usage of thwumps which many great DDA makers love to use is called the Moving Wall method.  It�s becoming more common, and was popularized by Lucidium�s Pressed for Time.  When placed in a specific area, half-way engulfed in a tile, a thwump is placed facing the inside of the wall.  See this diagram.



In the diagram above, as long as there is any tile in the red area, the thwump will move in the direction of the arrow when touched.  In the last two cases, a little more care is needed when placing the thwumps.  They will not obey the special �Moving Wall� rules if they are placed using 1/2 snap.  The third thwump in the diagram must be placed exactly one pixel above its 1/2 snap position to work.  The last thwump must be placed exactly one pixel left of its 1/2 snap position to work.  Both of these can be done by code-editing. Knowing that the 2 numbers directly after the 8^ represent the x- and y-coordinates of the object, you know how to manipulate its position to move it one pixel in the direction you want (you�ll be subtracting 1 to do this either way, from the x-coordinate for a leftward moving wall, or from the y-coordinate for an upward moving wall).  This is a map with plenty of moving walls to demonstrate their impact in a DDA.

From the DDA manifesto. Go figure.

Bits of the map were cool but here is some advice:

1) Firstly, don't make dead-end corridors that you die if you go down. It makes the game guess work, and makes it alot less fun.

2) In some places only half the number of mines were needed. Try to not place uneeded objects as it increases loading time.

3) If posting the level on the forums, post it in a .txt document, not a word document. It makes things easier for everyone!

Hope that helps.

#316744 2 2007-04-29T10:55:28Z lord_day
It's pretty simple. I assume you are talking about the glitch which makes some floorguards be able to travel over some doors, while others can't? Well if you place the door before you place the floorguard, then the floorguard will travel on it. If you place the door after you place the floorguard, then the floorguard won't travel on it. It's just a matter of placing floorguards and doors in the right order.

#327358 34 2007-06-04T16:05:58Z lord_day
Quote: Kablizzy, June 04, 2007 08:35 am
Simplicity - Minimalism makes packs. Long maps don't belong in map packs. Packs, more often than not, are meant to be played as a whole, rather than just a conglomeration of maps. If it were just a smattering of different maps, you'd be better suited to submit them to NUMA as a series.
I completely disagree with this point.

I think map packs with mainly simple maps are the packs that suffer the worst. Long maps can often be the maps that make a map pack memorable. Simple maps just fly by in a flurry, and don't leave a lasting impression. It's the maps that people spend time on, or introduce a new concept that really hold people. I think maps that are just 'simple' are usually fillers made to take up space so that a mapper can reach his target number of levels. A pack of simple maps can work, but that is to say, they usually work as a group of simple maps. I think it's the long maps that really make a pack what it is.
#327367 36 2007-06-04T16:36:07Z lord_day
There is a difference between short maps and simple maps, maybe which I didn't pick up on in my other post. I think mainly, the /simple/ maps are ones that bring a pack down. As opposed to short maps. Short maps can be awesome if executed well.

Quote: Blizz Meister K, 2007-06-04T18:29:20Z
Consistently, patterns, flow, themes, utilizing space, physics, and simplicity. That's how you make a MetaMap.

I agree with bits of the post that you made, but I think this is where I disagree with you the most. Metanet maps were the first maps, and alot of them are good. But I do not think at all that they are the best maps. A map does not have to resemble a metanet map to be a good map, and I think alot of the best maps are far from 'Metamaps.'

Also, I think anyone trying to write a course in map making is destined to fail, because almost everyone makes maps in a different way or style. You cannot just become a good mapper overnight by reading a guide to map making. The best way to become a good mapper is devolp your own method of making maps, and hone it to perfection. Players who have done this have become the legends of N, and I think people trying to copy other people's styles of mapping always fall short of the mark.

And martyr, give me about a week and a half, and I'll name the 50 best levels from your pack for you.

#448857 175 2008-06-26T15:31:00Z lord_day
I think I may have a better scoring system:

A player:
Gains 1000 points for every gym he beats.
Loses 100 points for every failed attempt at beating a gym.
Gains 200 points for every scrimmage he wins.
Loses 100 points for every scrimmage he loses.
Gains 700 points for becoming Champion.
Loses 100 points if the Champion beats him after he challenged him.

The Champion:
Gains 300 points for every challenger he beats.
Loses 500 points if he loses to a Challenger.


I don't think all golds should be any points, as they are part of beating a gym. Otherwise they are a double reward. And perhaps all players should start with 1000 points to prevent negative points happening.

#21531 1 2008-09-18T21:07:21Z lord_day
The Continued Column

So. It's time for me to release the sequel to my level pack, The Completed Column, and now seems to be the perfect time to do so. I've been working on this sequel for over half a year, and like all things, it comes with a back story that I won't bore you with. This pack has 51 levels, most of which are easy except for the last two episodes. They are hard. Please, post any demos you have.

This pack has one dedication, and it is to Jerrod, aka rocket_thumped. He is an awesome guy and went through two rounds of playtesting this pack for me. Without him, it wouldn't be half as good. So for that, he gains the dedication of 13-2: Roll High Little One. He deserves it.

Enjoy all.
